# src/units/ — one fragment per landed unit

A unit is a set of reconstructed translation units that landed together. Its fragment, `<name>.mk`, is a piece of
Makefile that `src/Makefile` reads: it appends the unit's sources to `ECOURNA_SRCS` (paths relative to
`src/ecourna`, compiled with `ECOURNA_CXXFLAGS`) or `UENUX2_SRCS` (paths relative to `src/uenux2`, compiled with
`UENUX2_CXXFLAGS`, without `-fno-strict-aliasing` in the directories and files `UENUX2_TBAA_DIRS` lists but the files
`UENUX2_NOTBAA_FILES` lists, `docs/reference/facts/app-flags.md` §F6, the object keeping the path), and adds whatever
the unit needs beyond that: prerequisites the generic rules lack, a compile rule of its own in the rare case the
generic one gives the wrong flags, and companion objects appended to `EXTRA_OBJS`. The build machinery the fragments
rely on (the ecourna flags every unit gets, the ASN.1 runtime, the splat build) is in `src/mk/`.

```
ECOURNA_SRCS += api/security/csha.cpp
UENUX2_SRCS += src/api/util/cwait.cpp
```

## What a fragment holds

`07-ecourna-app-dados.mk` has items 1, 2 and 4, in the order fragments write them; `08-ecourna-app-dados-midias.mk` has
item 3 as well:

1. A header comment naming the classes and the target functions the unit reconstructs. A score it quotes is `pnpm
   wasm score <target> <index> <link> <ours>` at the default depth; one taken with `--depth 0` says so.
2. The sources: `DADOS_SRCS := app/dados/tiposbasicos.cpp …` then `ECOURNA_SRCS += $(DADOS_SRCS)`. A variable of its
   own lets the next parts, and the `*_TRY` switches below, name the unit's files.

   A unit can have no source of its own. When every function it matches is code the compiler emits from a class
   definition (the implicit destructors emitted with the vtable, an inline default of a virtual member) and the
   class's other members are pending or not written, the fragment holds only its companion rules, the class
   definition goes in a header they include, and the `.cpp` the records name, if any, stays a stub-only file that
   `make -C src stubs` rewrites. Unit 19 has only a roots rule, which emits and address-takes
   `IScreen::GetFontMetrics`, an inline default in `api/gui/iscreen.h`.

   A fragment can also be its header comment and nothing else, with its row in the table. That is the shape when a
   header edit is the whole unit and every landed TU that includes the header emits the code (unit 94), and when the
   unit is the ASN.1 runtime's: `src/mk/asn1.mk` compiles every `src/asn1/*.cxx` by wildcard, so a fragment there lists
   no source (unit 85; units 86 and 87 add exclusions, and unit 87 a roots rule), and when the unit's code is a tree
   root (`src/app/roots_*.cpp`, whose rule and `APP_OBJS` entry are in `src/Makefile`, since a fragment's objects link
   after both trees): the fragment holds the evidence and the root's comment cites it (unit 166). `make -n -C src link`
   counts 0 compiles for it. So does a TSE source whose linkonce copies must win over the tree roots' (a file at
   another tree's rule): its rule and its place go in `src/Makefile`, in the variable of the setup whose flags it has,
   and the fragment holds the evidence (unit 253's `mock/app/comum/cappinfobuilder.cpp` in `VOTA_WEB_OBJS`; placed
   after the tree roots it gained 12 of the 17 rows it gains second, first in `EXTRA_OBJS` none).
3. A compile rule of its own, when the generic one gives the wrong flags: a `define …_RULE` template instantiated
   per source with `$(foreach u,$(DADOS_MIDIAS_SRCS),$(eval $(call …)))`, compiling from `/work/src/…` (below). Most units need none, and unit 08's only repeats a flag
   the tree has. A uenux2 source's own rule takes `$(call uenux2_cxxflags,<path under src/uenux2>)` where the generic
   rule takes it, not `$(UENUX2_CXXFLAGS)`, or it keeps `-fno-strict-aliasing` in a directory built without it
   (`src/Makefile`, `docs/reference/facts/app-flags.md` §F6). Its prerequisites are the source, `$(ROOT)/src/Makefile`, the fragment
   and every header the compile reads. Do not write `$(ECOURNA_HDRS)` or `$(UENUX2_HDRS)` there: `src/Makefile`
   includes the fragments before it sets those two variables, and a rule's prerequisites are expanded when the rule
   is read, so in a fragment both are empty and the rule depends on no header at all, with every check still
   passing. Compute the set in the fragment with the same command the generic rules use, `$(shell find
   $(ROOT)/src/uenux2 $(ROOT)/src/ecourna -name '*.h' -o -name '*.hpp')`, or name the directories the compile reads
   with `$(wildcard …)` (unit 40's companion rules list `$(wildcard $(ROOT)/src/uenux2/src/api/gui/*.h)` and the
   ecourna headers they include), plus `$(wildcard $(ROOT)/src/asn1/*.h)` when it includes `asn1/asn1.h`, which
   neither tree's set holds (§U7 has the make probe). Then an edit or a flag change here rebuilds the objects. A hand
   list of headers misses the ones they include: unit 36's pending rule names `src/asn1/asn1.h` but not the
   `asn1_useful.h` it includes (`docs/reference/facts/units.md` §U7).

   The generic ecourna rule in `src/Makefile` makes each object depend on its own source, at any depth, and on every
   `src/ecourna` header, as the generic uenux2 rule does for its tree. Neither generic rule depends on a `src/asn1`
   header, the modules' included: an edit of `asn1/asn1.h` rebuilds an object that includes it only through a rule
   that names `src/asn1` (§U7), so touch such a unit's sources after one, or do what units 04, 09 and 16 do: a rule
   with prerequisites and no recipe, which adds the `src/asn1` headers to the objects and leaves the generic rule to
   compile them. Unit 15's rule of that kind names its fragment and the ecourna headers but no `src/asn1` header, so an
   `asn1.h` edit misses its converter too, which includes it (§U7 lists what such an edit rebuilds, and the command
   that lists the objects it misses). The other way round, a header a unit edits or adds rebuilds every object of its
   tree, whether it includes the header or not, with `src/app/roots_uenux2.o` and every companion whose rule names the
   header's directory (`docs/reference/facts/workflow.md` §W15 counts them).
   `src/app/roots_ecourna.o` is not among them: its rule names no header, so after an edit of an ecourna header it
   includes (`final` on a class it `make_shared`s), touch `src/app/roots_ecourna.cpp`, or its old copy links on and a
   clean build disagrees with yours. A check that the unit's objects are fresh moves all of them aside, not only the
   unit's own: in the unit's worktree, `find build/out/app -name '*.o' -newer .git` lists every object it built.

   Check it with `make -n -C src link -W $PWD/src/ecourna/<source> | grep -c 'em++.* -c '`: 1 means an edit
   rebuilds the source's object. For a header or a fragment, count the object you care about: `make -n -C src link
   -W $PWD/<header> | /usr/bin/grep -c -- '-o [^ ]*<object>'` prints 1 when an edit of the header rebuilds it. The
   generic ecourna and uenux2 rules do not depend on any fragment: after changing a flag that only a target-specific
   variable carries, touch the unit's sources. A companion rule that names its fragment does depend on it, and
   `roots_asn1.cpp` depends on every fragment, so `make -n -C src link -W $PWD/src/units/<fragment>.mk` lists the
   companions and `roots_asn1.o` even for an edit of a comment.

   A rule of the fragment's own compiles the TSE source from its `/work/src/…` path, as the generic ecourna and
   uenux2 rules do: the tree's `-ffile-prefix-map` (`UENUX2_PREFIX_MAP`, `ECOURNA_PREFIX_MAP`) rewrites only that
   spelling, so the file names in the `std::source_location` records become the target's. A relative `src/…` path
   is never rewritten, and `make -C src check-srcloc` fails with `UNMAPPED` on such a record.
4. A rule for each companion file (next section) and `override EXTRA_OBJS += $(APP)/<fragment>-roots.o`. `override`
   keeps the companions in the link when `tools/round/try-tu.sh` passes its own `EXTRA_OBJS` on the make command line.
   A companion rule's prerequisites are item 3's, with the companion file for the source: the companion file,
   `$(ROOT)/src/Makefile`, the fragment, and the header set when the file includes anything (a roots file that only
   declares through `__asm__` labels needs no header). The set covers what the file's headers include too: unit 14's
   roots rule names `api/exception/*.hpp` because `eueguierror.h` includes `cbaseerror.hpp`. Unit 19's roots rule
   names its fragment and unit 01's does not, so an edit of `01-uenux2-api-util-io-ipc.mk` rebuilds no companion of
   unit 01's (§U7). A matched function compiled in a companion object is only as fresh as that rule.

A fragment holds no flag that every unit of its tree needs; those go in `src/mk/ecourna-flags.mk` or in
`src/Makefile` (`docs/reference/app-flags.md` lists them with their evidence). Comments cite the facts that decided a
flag as `docs/reference/facts/app-flags.md §F5`. Inside the Latin-1 trees, `src/uenux2` and `src/ecourna`, drop the
sign (`docs/reference/facts/reading.md R14`): a tool that writes UTF-8 saves it as two bytes, and `check_encoding.py`
then fails the file. The files of `src/units/` are UTF-8 (ASCII is UTF-8), but the externs file `.editorconfig`
declares Latin-1, a TSE source: a fragment quotes a target literal with its accents, in UTF-8. No `.editorconfig`
section covers the rest, so `check_encoding.py` checks none of them, and eleven fragments and a pending file are Latin-1
(`docs/reference/facts/units.md` §U14 has the census). A script that reads and writes Latin-1, the safe edit of a TSE
source, writes Latin-1 into a fragment too: run §U14's census over the files you edited. A flag that one function
seems to need is decided for its tree;
a fragment carries it only when the evidence is per TU (`docs/how-to-decompile.md` step 4, "When only a flag closes
the gap"). `-fno-PIC` looked per file until the target's data showed otherwise (§F5).

An include path is not a flag of the code (`asmlift-wasm/benchmark/README.md` "The compile setups"). A unit whose
headers reach a library path its tree's flags lack adds the path to its own objects with a target-specific variable:
unit 66's uenux2 TU includes ecourna's SQL headers, which include Boost, so its fragment has
`$(ROOT)/$(APP)/uenux2/$(CJUSTIFICADORDAO_SRCS).o: UENUX2_CXXFLAGS += -I$(BOOST_HDR)` and the same path on its pending
rule. Its objects keep their tree's setup. A merge can bring the need with no conflict: a header one side turns from a
layout stand-in into the real class can reach a library the other side's objects lack (unit 88 gives `ccargos.cpp` and
`cconfiguracaoeleicao.cpp` Boost's path, which they reach through `CConfiguracaoMunicipio`'s `CHorariosUrna`). The fix
is the fragment's include path, not the header.

A flag a fragment does add gives its objects a flag set, and
the benchmark's registry `asmlift-wasm/benchmark/dataset/setups.json` must have a setup with exactly that set.
Otherwise, from `asmlift-wasm/`, `pnpm bench setups --check` reports the tree's
setup as drift and `pnpm test:bench` fails; every splat, status and stubs check still passes, so run it before
the commit. A new setup is a new entry in `setups.json` and a re-selection of the dataset, which gives it the 5 rows
`test:bench` wants (`asmlift-wasm/benchmark/README.md` "Adding a row"), then a new baseline. That holds for a tree's
`make` setup. A library a fragment compiles takes a setup of its own naming its objects' variable, ahead of any
`artefact` setup whose variable they join: one appended to `RHVOICE_OBJS`, as zlib's and libqrencode's are, is
otherwise the `rhvoice` setup's at RHVoice's flags, and the check passes (`docs/reference/facts/libraries.md` §B11).

## Names

`NN-<component>-<what>.mk`:

- `NN`, two digits and from 100 on three, is the order `src/Makefile` reads the fragments in, and so the order their
  objects take in `ECOURNA_SRCS`, `UENUX2_SRCS` and `EXTRA_OBJS`, and their flags in `ECOURNA_CXXFLAGS`. It is the names'
  text order, so `100-…` sorts between `10-…` and `11-…`, not last, and a filter over the companion objects spells the
  number `[0-9]+`. That order reaches the link:
  wasm-ld numbers functions in input order, and binaryen's inlining can depend on it. The order of the sources inside
  one fragment can decide a body's locals, and so can the order of definitions inside one `.cpp` where the records' lines
  leave room (`docs/reference/facts/codegen.md` §C4). A body that differs only in its locals is worth one link with a
  fragment's sources swapped before a source form is hunted. A new fragment takes the next free number; renumbering a
  landed one changes the link. A new file whose order against a landed unit's file decides a body goes into that unit's
  list, on the side it measures right, not into a fragment whose number sorts on the other side: unit 49 lists
  `cwasmtimerscheduler.cpp` after `cwasmtimer.cpp`, and its fragment gives the two scores. A function whose file a
  landed unit already compiles takes no number: it extends that unit ("Landing a unit"). When several people or agents
  land units at the same time, each takes its number before it starts: in a run of several agents the coordinator hands
  them out (`docs/agents.md`), and otherwise the number is said in the pull request or issue that announces the work.
  The same holds for a landed file a new function needs on the other side of another landed unit's file: move it into
  that unit's list, on the side it measures right (the second fragment no longer lists it, "Landing a unit" item 2),
  and say so in both fragments (`docs/reference/facts/codegen.md` §C4 measures both moves). The move can rename a
  twin's match line, which step 9 does. A file in another unit's list stays its own unit's: its fragment keeps the
  header comment, the companions and the row, with no source list when every source moved, and says which list
  compiles its files; that list's fragment says why, with the measurement (unit 188's `simulador.cpp` sits in unit
  101's list for 4879, unit 245's three files in unit 96's). A new file whose functions only another unit's matches
  carry (a constructor twin, a callee binaryen inlines), and whose order was measured to decide nothing, goes in that
  unit's list beside its caller, and the fragment names the variant link that showed it (unit 197's `ccedula.cpp`).
  Whoever lands second has a link the first never measured: it runs link, census, symbols, match and the splat build
  again on top of the first (`docs/how-to-decompile.md` step 10, "Landing onto a moved main").
- `<component>` is the `src/` tree the sources live in: `ecourna`, `uenux2` or `asn1`, or the library's name for a unit
  that holds no tree's source (`103-rhvoice-nocatch`: a flag, a roots file and names, all RHVoice's).
- `<what>` is the source directory the unit's files share, with `/` as `-` (`api-security`, `app-dados-midias`), or
  the class when the unit is one class and what it calls (`cthreadvota`, `crdvvota`, `ctextfieldmultiline`). A uenux2
  path is spelled from under `src/uenux2/src/` or `src/uenux2/mock/app/` (`196-uenux2-api-util-cstringutils`,
  `127-uenux2-simulador-wasm-platform`). When the shared directory is already another fragment's name
  (`app/dados/asn/` and `app/dados/processoeleitoral/` share `app/dados`, which is 07's), use the class
  (`cconversorhorariosurna`); when other fragments' names extend it with a word (`152-uenux2-app-vota-operador-estados`,
  `161-…-identidade`), take the directory and a word of your own for the unit's classes, not one class's name. When
  several classes' files share no directory, use the directory that holds most of them and a word for what they are
  (`simulador-wasm-devices`, unit 81: four files in `mock/app/simulador/wasm/` and one in `src/api/util/`), or a
  family's name (`93-uenux2-estadoaplicacao`: `asn/estadoaplicacao/`, `md/estadoaplicacao/` and one file of
  `relatorios/`).

A fragment's companion files carry its name with a suffix: `-roots.cpp`, `-pending.cpp`, `-externs.cpp` (below).
A file compiled through `ECOURNA_SRCS` cannot have `_` in its name: the ecourna rule turns `_` back into `/`.

## Companion files

**Roots** (`-roots.cpp`). Until a unit's real callers are reconstructed, nothing in the skeleton calls it, and
wasm-ld drops what nothing references. A roots TU stands in for those callers, and it must reach each function the
way the target does, because binaryen's inlining and dead-argument removal depend on the callers it sees
(`docs/reference/facts/tool-traps.md` §X1):

- a function the target reaches through the table has its address taken. For one the target reaches only through a
  vtable, take the vtable's address: `extern const char x_vt[] __asm__("_ZTV…")`, stored into a `volatile` sink from a
  static constructor (units 15, 16, 17, 20 and 36). That is the reference the target has, the vptr store of a
  constructor not written yet, and it keeps every slot. Once that constructor is in the link (written and reached, if
  only inlined into another unit's function through a `GetInst`), the sink stands for nothing and goes, with its
  `extern` and its words in the roots comment, as a caller stand-in goes. A function reached through another kind of
  slot (an
  `invoke_*` thunk, a callback passed by its slot) has its own address taken, and so can a vtable member that also has
  direct callers, which keeps the vtable its vptr store names. `ledger/callgraph.tsv` holds
  direct calls only, so such a function can look like one with a single caller; `docs/reference/facts/units.md` §U11
  lists who passes its slot to an `invoke_*`;
- a function with direct calls in the target gets at least two call sites in our link, because binaryen inlines a
  function called once: two callers, or one caller that calls it twice (the target's 2761 calls 5457 twice, and unit
  32's stand-in did the same until 2761 landed). The reconstructed callers count: the roots TU adds `noinline` ones for the rest.
  Each stand-in's comment names the target callers it stands for. A stand-in beside a real caller compiles, links and
  passes every check, so a search of those indices is what says it can go, at a merge too: in `splat/matched.txt`, and in
  the scan for a caller written but not matched, since a caller in the link ends a stand-in whether or not its row
  matches (unit 08's `UsaFaseDeconverte` stood for 9052 and went while 9052 was written and `differs`). A written caller
  with no symbol line reads `no-candidate` with no object in the scan, as if not written (4349 did): look its symbol up
  in `build/out/wasm-map/vota_web/names.tsv`, and its call in our link (how-to step 10's `wasm-objdump` awk). A library
  caller can be in our link with the callee inlined into it, its row `no-candidate` all the same (SQLite's `unixSleep`,
  12902, holds `nanosleep`, 6265): look a library caller's name up in `build/out/wasm-map/vota_web/names.tsv` before
  writing a stand-in for it. A stand-in that reads the callee's result stands for a reader too, and goes only when a
  real reader is in the link, or binaryen drops the result and the callers that drop it lose a row: unit 123's
  `w123_copia` reads 1249's returned `this`, as the target's 11395 does, where 5856 and 11197 drop it. A stand-in goes
  when every caller it stands for is in the link, not when the link first holds two real call sites: one whose callers
  landed in part stays, and its comment says which still wait. A caller counts when our link holds its call: one in the
  link that inlines the callee where the target calls it ends nothing, and the comment says so (unit 190's two
  `GetPathLog` calls stay for 8302, whose copy inlines it). A stand-in may go earlier only with a link without it that
  moves no row (`docs/reference/facts/workflow.md` §W13, "One variant object in the whole link"), measured and said in
  the fragment. Scanned with the committed symbol file, such a link counts the rows under the names they have now; for
  the set a fragment quotes, run steps 7 to 9 on the variant link with private paths
  (`docs/reference/facts/app-flags.md` §F9 item 6 gives the options). A stand-in that only keeps a library callee of the
  unit out of line, as the target does, stays only when a row moves with it (unit 214's for 3373 and 3372 moved none).
  The rule goes call by call: in a stand-in that calls several callees, a call whose callee's every target caller is in
  the link goes, measured with steps 6 to 10, and the stand-in stays for its other calls (unit 31's `TelaMTA` and
  `TelaMTB` called ten adders; their calls of 728, 941, 5409 and 5419, whose callers are all in the link, went). A
  stand-in for one target caller, whatever units its callees are in, goes whole when that caller lands; each callee's
  fragment says where its call site is. One in a file another active stage claims is left to the landing, with a link
  without it measured and its lines named in the result (how-to step 10). 5629 has two direct callers in the target
  (`awk -F'\t' '$2==5629' ledger/callgraph.tsv` prints 5324 and 11387); unit 20 reconstructs 11387, so `20-…-roots.cpp`
  adds one caller standing in for 5324. binaryen inlines a function for having one call only when no table entry or
  export refers to it (`docs/reference/facts/tool-traps.md` §X1), so a function the table also holds keeps its body with
  a single caller. A function with one direct caller in the target that keeps its body all the same (11514, 6868 bytes,
  under 2543: `awk -F'\t' '$2==11514' ledger/callgraph.tsv`) had a second call the target's later merges took away (X1:
  a function can end with one caller and keep its body). Its caller inlines it in our link once written, so a stand-in
  gives the second call site; its comment names the one caller, says the merged-away call is a reading (X1), and says
  what would identify that caller (a twin of the caller, a merge's other members). It goes once that caller is
  identified and in the link. A destructor whose other target caller is `__shared_ptr_emplace<T>::__on_zero_shared` gets
  that caller only from a live `std::make_shared<T>` call, which needs T's constructor: while the constructor is not
  written, the vtable's address is enough, and the control block's members come from a builder the link drops ("A
  control block") or wait for the unit that writes it;
- a function the target inlines into its only caller is left unrooted, so it is inlined here too. It can be a
  function the source defined out of line that binaryen inlined, which `docs/how-to-decompile.md` step 3 tells from
  the caller's body: write it out of line, in the file that step says, and leave it unrooted. When such a function
  passes an internal function's table slot (its only reference in the target) and its own caller is not written,
  give it a caller stand-in that calls it once, so it inlines there as in the target and the slot keeps its function
  (unit 123's `w123_titulo`, for 11946, keeps `TituloPartido`, 11181). The ASN.1 runtime's
  roots are generated and take the address of every external function, so a fragment takes such a function out with
  `ASN1_ROOTS_EXCLUDE += <part of its mangled name>` (`src/asn1/README.md` "How it is built");
- the stand-ins differ in their bytes, from each other and from every target function. binaryen merges identical
  functions: two identical stand-ins become one caller and the function is inlined into it. A stand-in that only
  forwards, `return D2(p);`, is the D2 of a derived class that adds no member (vota::CAssinadorVota's 7766), which the
  census pairs with it and step 9 would match under the stand-in's name (`docs/reference/facts/units.md` §U2). Give
  each one a body no target function has: two calls (unit 34), an offset added to an argument (unit 23's
  `bloco + 12`). Before step 8, read the census's `moved:` list (`build/out/census/progress.md`) for a target
  function paired with a `-roots.cpp` symbol. A stand-in's own use of a library template instantiates it in the roots
  object, where the census can pair it too (a string `==` there paired with 1148): use what instantiates nothing
  (`size()`). A stand-in that calls a function with a defaulted `std::source_location`
  passes an empty one by name (unit 100's `w100_usa`): the default makes a record on the stand-in's own line, which
  `check_srcloc.py --strict` prints as `MOVED`. When every caller of the function in the link is a stand-in, an empty
  location is one constant at every call, and dead-argument elimination folds it into the callee: the stand-in then
  takes the location as a parameter of its own, address-taken so nothing folds it, and passes it on (unit 186's
  `w186_erro` did, until 2725, its one target caller, landed). When every target caller holds one inlined throw, so the
  callee's wasm type lacks the location, the stand-in calls the function that holds the throw, which binaryen inlines
  into it: the record is then the one of every call site (unit 205's `w205_assinatura` calls `VerificaUrnaDesligando`
  for 1685);
- a constant every target caller passes is not in the target's wasm type: binaryen's dead-argument elimination folded
  it into the body (`docs/how-to-decompile.md` step 2). Pass that constant from every stand-in, so the fold happens
  here too, and vary what the target's callers vary, or binaryen folds that as well: unit 26's stand-ins both passed
  the `ESavdAplic` 1, and unit 28's pass the first `TColor` as 2 and the second as 1 and 0. The varied call has to
  survive clang, which inlines a small operator at a hot site and keeps a call only in a cold block: unit 100's
  `w100_falha` is `[[noreturn]]` and `cold`, attributes a stand-in may have and a landed source may not. The constant
  must be one when dead-argument elimination runs: a value read through a call binaryen inlines later (an accessor out
  of line in another object) is not, and keeps the parameter, which is also how a callee keeps one the target keeps
  (stand-ins for 653's callers that pass `GetAreaTela()` leave it the parameter the target folded, and ones that name
  `CApplication::s_areaTela` do not; unit 31's 3678 reads `GetAreaTela()`, so 5544 keeps its parameter as the target's
  does);
- the roots TU declares what it calls through `__asm__` labels with the object's signatures, before binaryen:
  `extern "C" int f(int, int) __asm__("_ZN7ecourna…")`. A constructor and a non-deleting destructor return `this`
  there, so they are declared `int` whatever the target's type or the stub says, and a caller that drops the result
  lets binaryen remove it as the target's did (unit 23). A label whose result differs from the object's, a constructor
  declared `void` or a `void` member declared `int`, links with `wasm-ld: warning: function signature mismatch: <symbol>`
  among the link's other warnings, routes the calls to a trap stub and can leave the function one caller, which
  binaryen inlines (§U1). The stub is a `signature_mismatch:` row of `build/out/wasm-map/vota_web/names.tsv`: count them
  before and after (`/usr/bin/grep -c 'signature_mismatch:' build/out/wasm-map/vota_web/names.tsv` prints 0 at
  4487d6b). A label spells every parameter's type, so a header that changes one breaks it at link time. A function
  that returns a class by value (a `std::shared_ptr` too) takes the result's address first and returns nothing, `void
  f(void* resultado, …)` (how-to step 2; units 161's and 193's roots). A constructor whose result a target caller
  reads needs no label: `new (buffer) T(…)`'s result is its returned `this` (unit 136, §U13). A label that names a
  function the same TU defines (an explicit instantiation in a tree root) is that function to clang, which can inline
  it into the stand-in: `[[clang::noinline]]` on each call statement keeps the calls (`roots_vector.cpp`, §U13), an
  attribute a stand-in may carry. There clang also compares the label with the definition: a `bool` parameter or
  result declared `int` gives no warning but a local `<symbol>_bitcast_invalid` trap that every call through the label
  reaches, and binaryen drops what follows the calls. Spell such types as the definition does, and check the object:
  `wasm-objdump -x build/out/app/roots_iconversorasn.o | /usr/bin/grep -c bitcast_invalid` prints 0;
- the roots TU supplies no copy of a template or an inline member that should come from another object: wasm-ld keeps
  the first object's copy of a linkonce (COMDAT) function, and the roots objects come after the units' sources and
  before the ASN.1 runtime's objects and the libraries (`EXTRA_OBJS` in `LINK_OBJS`, `src/Makefile`). So what a roots
  file instantiates that a unit's object instantiates too still comes from the unit's object, and what the roots file
  instantiates first is its copy. A stand-in that has to call a template a later object or a library owns declares
  it `extern template`: the object then has `U` for it and emits nothing. That line belongs in the roots file, not in
  a TSE header, where it would be text whose only purpose is to steer the compiler. It keeps a roots file's copy out
  of the link, and nothing more: every real TU that instantiates the template emits its own copy at its own level.
  A TSE header holds one only on the target's evidence: every target caller of the template calls it out of line
  where clang would inline it, and the kept copy is one object's (its record). The header then declares it, that
  object's `.cpp` instantiates it, and the fragment says what shows it (unit 112: `CBaseType<…>`'s constructor, 2666,
  which 3939 and 9116 call with constants, in `tiposbasicos.hpp`). A class-level `extern template class` keeps every
  out-of-line member out of the roots object, so a member the root itself calls that no later object defines stops
  the link (`undefined symbol`): an explicit instantiation of that member in the same root is the route
  (`roots_boost.cpp`'s `perl_matcher::match_imp`). Taking a root's copies out can also take copies of other templates
  only they instantiated, so read again every row the root's object matched (step 9's `join`, step 10's `comm`). A
  non-template inline member has no such line: a root keeps another object's copy of it only by not using it, and its
  callers' and callees' rows are read after (`roots_boost.cpp`'s probe no longer calls `connection_body_base::
  disconnect()`, so 9570 and 9578 match from ecourna's `-fexceptions` copies). The library probes the tree roots
  include (`tools/libs/boost/probe.cpp`, `tools/libs/7zip/driver.cpp`, `tools/libs/rhvoice/driver.cpp`) are stand-ins
  under these rules, a call the target lacks included, and an edit of one changes what that library's `build.sh`
  builds for its version study (`docs/reference/facts/libraries.md` §B7).

  The tree roots are different: `src/app/roots_*.o` are in `APP_OBJS`, after the executable TU's object and ahead of
  both trees, so what they instantiate is the copy the link keeps unless that TU instantiates it too (a fragment's
  roots link after both trees and cannot). A tree
  root holds an instance of a header's template, never a body written there: a specialization's body is a stand-in's
  own, and it outlives a real copy with no word from the link.
  - **Whose copy.** A record says it (the installed header's path), and so does a landing pad (an `-fexceptions`
    object's). An instance with neither is the tree's whose copy, compiled at each tree's flags, equals the target's body, and that
    has a target caller calling it directly (its `component` or vtable in `pnpm wasm fn`): uenux2's for
    `vector<char>::__insert_with_size` (1927), which comum's 11397 and 11399 call. The root's comment names those
    callers, as a stand-in's does, and the instance goes when one of them lands and its object is the first on the
    link line to instantiate it, where a caller stand-in waits for all of its callers (above): 1927 comes from 11397's
    object now. A landed caller whose object links behind another tree's user of the template ends nothing, so the
    comment says what does. When the target holds the instance only merged into another instance's body (one function
    in both instances' table slots), the partner's tree is the instance's: the comment names the partner, the slots
    and the instance's callers in any tree (1158, `vector<unsigned char>::__assign_with_size`, in `roots_vector.cpp`,
    merged with `vector<char>`'s). A root's linkonce copy can also shadow a tree object's copy the target keeps (a
    landing pad says so): an `extern template` line in the root lets that copy link, before a root at other flags is
    considered (`roots_boost.cpp`'s for 1376 and 9415, which ecourna's objects give), and the root's object is compared
    before and after (`docs/reference/facts/tool-traps.md` §X12).
    uenux2 has two flag sets, with and without strict aliasing (`UENUX2_TBAA_DIRS`), and a copy can be one set's where
    no written object of that set gives it: `roots_set.cpp` holds `std::set<std::string>`'s hinted insert (2281) without
    strict aliasing, which the object that brought it, unit 268's, no longer emits with the flag, and which its TSE
    caller's directory (`src/app/vota/`) would not give either, a reading. Such a root stands for an object not
    identified, and goes once that object is written; its place follows the callees it brings (last in `APP_OBJS`, after
    `roots_rhvoice.o`, whose `__tree_balance_after_insert`, 152, is the target's: unit 268's fragment).
    A root compiles at the flags of the object whose copy the target keeps, since it brings its callees' linkonce
    copies to the head of the link too, and that object can be another tree's than the template's:
    `roots_cbasetype.cpp`, at `UENUX2_CXXFLAGS` right after `roots_uenux2.o`, for an ecourna template whose target
    copies are uenux2's (unit 112), its rule depending on the ecourna headers, and `roots_iconversorasn.cpp` after it
    for `IConversorASN`'s members (unit 166), its rule on the ASN.1 module headers too. The other way round, uenux2's
    objects link ahead of ecourna's, so a uenux2 TU that is a library template's first user takes the copies
    ecourna's objects gave the link: one that uses `std::regex` takes the regex internals, of which the target holds
    two sets: the `const char*` parser's, the token iterator's and `std::regex_replace`'s are uenux2's code without
    strict aliasing or landing pads (`roots_cbasetype.cpp` holds the last two; the parser's come from
    `app/comum/dados/cqrcodeds.cpp`'s object, built so and linked ahead of `cinputmenufield.cpp`'s), and the
    `std::string` constructor's are an `-fexceptions` object's, which ecourna's `api/compression/icompressor.cpp` gives
    (unit 06's fragment; a root at `ECOURNA_CXXFLAGS` held them until that object's caller, 5821, was in the link).
    A tree root at flags other than `APP_CXXFLAGS` takes a rule of its own, as each of them has (`roots_cbasetype.o`'s
    and `roots_set.o`'s at `UENUX2_CXXFLAGS`), and every tree root a place in `APP_OBJS`, both in `src/Makefile`, whose
    edit rebuilds every object. wasm-ld keeps the first copy, so a new root lists the functions it defines in common
    with the roots ahead of it and goes after the one whose copy the target keeps (`P=build/out/<handle>`, then for each
    object `TMPDIR=/tmp build/docker-build.sh /emsdk/upstream/bin/llvm-nm --defined-only -j build/out/app/<root>.o |
    sort -u > $P/<root>.txt`, and `comm -12` of two lists: `roots_cbasetype.o` and `roots_vector.o` share
    `__throw_length_error` and `length_error`'s constructor). To measure a root before that edit, compile it into a
    clone of `APP` (§W13) and link into a private `OUT` and `OUT_NAMED` with `"APP_OBJS=$(make -s -C src print-APP_OBJS)
    <root>.o"` on the make line, which links the existing object; `make -s -C src print-LINK_OBJS` with the same
    override shows its place. On the default `OUT` with a current link, make links nothing and steps 7 to 9 measure the
    plain link (`docs/reference/facts/workflow.md` §W26): check that the link's `link.cmd` holds the new order.
  - **The executable TU's copies.** Its `-Oz` code without landing pads comes from an explicit instantiation with
    `__attribute__((minsize))`, the header's own body (`roots_vector.cpp`, below), at uenux2's flags when the body
    loads across a store, since that TU compiles without strict aliasing, which `docs/reference/facts/levels.md` §V1
    measures. A copy with landing pads (an `invoke_*` import in its body) needs `-fexceptions`: an explicit
    instantiation in a tree root at `VOTA_WEB_CXXFLAGS` (`roots_texttospeech.cpp`'s `has<api::ITextToSpeech>`, 3281). A
    class with no key function whose inline members are that TU's copies takes "A class with no key function" (below)
    in such a root: an unrooted `optnone` builder, as the TU's code builds the class (`roots_texttospeech.cpp`, unit
    237). `minsize` reaches the instantiated function only. A callee the `-Oz` copy calls out of line and the
    instance would inline takes a `minsize` instantiation of its own (an instance of 2247 calls `~basic_string` as the
    target does only with `__destroy_at` and `allocator_traits::destroy` instantiated so too), which the try object
    shows (`wasm-objdump -d`), not a lone score. An implicit special member cannot be named so: `std::pair`'s
    destructor, which its header does not declare, stops the compile (`explicit instantiation refers to member function
    … that is not an instantiation`), so its `-Oz` copy (1928) comes only from
    code at that TU's rule that instantiates it implicitly (unit 253's `cappinfobuilder.cpp` now), and so does a copy
    that
    calls it out of line (3552, which `~CoderEnv` calls, from `roots_coderenv.cpp`'s instances of a header's template that
    destroy a `CoderEnv`, unit 229; `docs/reference/facts/asn1.md` §A10). `roots_vector.cpp` holds the TU's copies of any template
    (`string::find_first_not_of` too). An out-of-line callee is counted like any function, so it needs its
    target callers in the link too: `roots_vector.cpp` instantiates 306 and 520, the two callers 4967 has
    (`docs/reference/facts/splat.md` §S10 items 16 and 17). Read each callee's row after, since the copy brings its
    callees too. Once the executable TU's own code instantiates the template, its object, first on the link line, holds
    the copy and the stand-in goes: fn 252, `std::operator+(string&&, string&&)`, and 3717, `append`, came so from
    `roots_uenux2.cpp` until unit 170, and a landing reads both rows (`docs/reference/facts/splat.md` §S10).
  - **Two kinds of stand-in.** One that only instantiates keeps the copy the target has: its body equals the library
    object's (compare the rows, masked), and it leaves the link
    (`/usr/bin/grep -c urna_insere_doubles build/out/wasm-map/vota_web/names.tsv` prints 0; it keeps `vector<double>`'s
    range insert, 2662, with TBAA for RHVoice). An implicit or inline member of a plain class takes this kind, since
    no explicit instantiation names it: `::new (d) T(c);` keeps an implicit copy constructor out of line with no
    attribute (`roots_iconversorasn.cpp`'s `urna_copia_dados_comparecimento`, 5846). An explicit instantiation leaves
    none, and its instance stays in the link only while something calls it: one whose target callers are not written
    needs a caller stand-in in the same root, which `urna_roots` in `src/app/roots.cpp` calls (`urna_roots_vector`,
    `urna_roots_iconversorasn`), or which its address in a sink keeps, as fragment roots keep theirs; a single call
    from `urna_roots` lets binaryen inline it there. Once some of those callers are written, a stand-in for the rest
    may sit in the roots of a unit that writes one, under the fragment roots' rule (unit 150's `TemFala` stands for
    3281's caller 14478). A caller stand-in gives call sites, as the roots bullets above
    say, and its calls stay in whichever function binaryen left them (`roots_vector.cpp`'s `~vector` callers): check
    it by the calls, not by its name.
  - **`roots_ecourna.cpp`** is also unit 00's call-site roots, older than the companion files: a function added to
    unit 00's sources takes its stand-ins there (`UsaPreenche1` to `UsaPreenche3` for `CStringUtils::PreencheAEsquerda`;
    `UsaPreencheADireita` stood there for `PreencheADireita`'s callers until they were in the link).

  A roots file includes a unit's header for the declarations its stand-ins call (unit
  02), to give an inline member or function a uenux2 caller, and to emit code a landed header defines in one of the
  shapes below. Each shape stores only a vtable the
  target's own code stores, and defines no member of the class it builds that the unit's object does not define first.
  Each is listed with the match lines it gives in `docs/reference/facts/units.md` §U4:
  - **A control block.** For a class the target builds with `std::make_shared<T>`, the roots file includes T's header
    and calls `make_shared` (units 14, 27, 28, 29, 58, 65, 77, 79, 84 and 90), which instantiates `__shared_ptr_emplace<T>`.
    For one the target builds with `std::shared_ptr<U>(new T)`, its builder allocates the T and stores
    `__shared_ptr_pointer<T*, …>`'s vptr into a block of its own (3768 and 7787 for `CSqlConnection`, 11523 for
    `CFileSeeder`). The roots file then does the same (units 12 and 83). When T is a library type, the block goes in
    the roots of the unit whose header's inline code builds it (unit 03's for `boost::dynamic_bitset<uebyte>`, which
    `CBitArray`'s constructor builds: 9408, 9409). Or it address-takes a template stand-in that
    takes the `T*` (or a `std::unique_ptr<U>`) as its parameter and returns the `std::shared_ptr<U>`. That stand-in is
    never called and uses no constructor of T (units 78 and 79). The builder can also be one the link drops: an unrooted
    `optnone` function at namespace scope (in the anonymous namespace clang emits none of it, §U13) holds the
    `make_shared<T>` or the `shared_ptr<U>(new T)`, and the static constructor keeps the
    block's vtable in the sink (unit 83 for `__shared_ptr_pointer`, unit 92 for both kinds). That is sound because the
    target reaches these members only through that vtable, so the sink is the reference the target has, and no body
    depends on the constructor. Check each vtable with the grep for its address point below: the target's own code must
    store it, and the kind must be the one its builder stores. A dropped builder needs T's constructor declared, not
    defined, so it lifts "waits for the unit that writes it" (unit 92's masks). Its constructors are then compared with
    nothing, and the fragment says so. The two cross for a `__shared_ptr_pointer`: an unrooted `optnone` member of a
    class template that takes the `T*` and returns the `std::shared_ptr<U>`, one `template struct SBloco<U, T>;` per
    block (a template alone is instantiated by nothing), needs a head of T only and leaves nothing in the link (unit
    102, every pointer block). A `final` T whose destructor is out of line keeps a call to that D1 in the block, so the
    destructor must be in the link. Where the roots TU only declares that destructor, a
    `__shared_ptr_pointer<T*, default_delete<T>, …>` block's `__on_zero_shared` frees the D1's result, a reader the
    target lacks, and binaryen keeps the result in the destructor, which then differs: a block for `comum::CCalculaCV`
    matched 11262, its `__get_deleter`, and cost 5614, the destructor. Such a block waits for its real builder; before
    landing any block from a roots file, score each target function its slot 2 calls with and without it. Either way
    the roots object stores the control block's vtable, the one the target's builder stores. A `make_shared<T>` builder,
    dropped or not, also emits T's vtable and inline members as linkonce copies. A unit's source that emits them links
    first; otherwise, once any object keeps T's vtable, the first companion on the link line that has a copy gives
    them (`102-…` sorts before `79-…`), so read `names.tsv`'s object column before saying where a T member matches
    from. The control block's members are the target's and match from the roots object until T's
    real callers land. For `__shared_ptr_emplace<T>` those are the D0 and the D2, and `__on_zero_shared` too when the
    target's slot 2 is a body of its own and not the shared 411: unit 77's `final` parts have one (5966, 5969, 11958)
    and its other two hold 411, and 2967 and 7839 are two more, which match from their real builders. For
    `__shared_ptr_pointer<T*, …>` they are `__get_deleter` and, for unit 12, `__on_zero_shared` (§U3). `__asm__`-label stand-ins reach and match the same
    constructor (unit 23); the control block adds its own matches. When one of the block's real builders is in the link
    (written and reached, matched or not), its stand-in goes whole, since a block stand-in stands for the vtable
    reference, which any one builder's vptr store gives: the `SBloco` line or `w102_` builder, the `extern` of the
    block's vtable and its sink line (`git show cf7d70d1 -- src/units/102-uenux2-control-blocks-roots.cpp` drops the
    three for `CPreShowFormVota`'s block), and the fragment's count of blocks kept from the roots goes down by one.
  - **A class with no key function.** Its vtable is emitted only where the class is used
    (`docs/reference/facts/reading.md` §R6). Until a user lands, the roots file includes the class header and builds
    an object in an `optnone` function at namespace scope that nothing calls, which wasm-ld drops with the inlined
    constructor. It then takes the vtable's address as above (unit 67; units 75 and 83 build their objects as the target's 8302 and 11523
    do). A constructor written over a callee with no body is fine there: wasm-ld drops the function, and `make -C src
    link` prints no undefined symbol (unit 92's over `ITimerScheduler::GetInst`). An `optnone` function that calls the
    destructor by its qualified name emits the D2, the D0 and the vtable when the destructor destroys something, since
    it then stores the vptr (units 03, 11 and 92); take that route when the constructor cannot be written or would bring
    instances of its own into the roots object (a member's copy constructor, a control block). A destructor that
    destroys nothing (no member with a destructor, an empty body) is the base's D2 to clang, and nothing names the
    vtable (§U13): build an object there. A class template's instantiations are emitted the same way, each built as
    the target's callers build it (unit 79).
  - **An inline default of a virtual member.** A qualified call in an `optnone` function emits it, and an `__asm__`
    label takes its address (unit 19).
  - **A header's inline function the target calls directly**: a member of a class template
    (`CPolySingleton<T>::instance`, unit 100; `IConversorASN<E, D>::Converte`, unit 107), a member a plain class
    defines in its body, or a free `inline` function (744, `api::KeyName`). The stand-ins call it as the target's
    callers do, twice, and build the result in place, `::new (destino) ENTIDADE(conversor.Converte(dado));`, which by
    guaranteed elision emits no constructor or destructor of the result's type, inline members a later object
    defines. The object then emits TSE code, so the flags rule below applies (unit 113 compiles such roots at the
    tree's flags). A private member (libc++'s `__swap_out_circular_buffer`) cannot be called by name: the roots
    instantiate it explicitly and call it through an `__asm__` label with `[[clang::noinline]]` on each call, as a
    tree root does, and since that copy is the link's only while no object of ours instantiates the member, the roots
    compile at the flags of the target caller's directory (unit 225, 5700).

  Still forbidden: a stand-in whose body is a target function's (the stand-ins bullet above, §U2); a template or
  inline member a later object defines too, when the roots' copy would take the place of the ASN.1 runtime's or a
  library's (they come after the roots, so the roots' copy wins the link), unless `extern template` keeps it out or a
  tree root's copy is the same code; a control block the target's builder does not store (`make_shared` where the target
  stores `__shared_ptr_pointer`'s vptr, or the reverse); and any member of T beyond what the shape emits. A copy of a
  header's inline member may win over a later companion's, since both are a landed header's code, when the fragment shows
  that the two objects' flags give the same function (12090, `IResultado`'s D2, came from unit 102's roots ahead of unit
  60's pending object, until unit 122's `iresultado.cpp`, a real object linked ahead of both, emitted it). When the
  later object's copy is at another level than the target's (the runtime's `-Os` `__destroy_vector` over `Visitante`,
  where the target's 3552 is `-Oz`), no shape gives the target's copy: the member
  stays pending, a hand edit names it for its callers, and the fragment gives the probe, until code at the target copy's
  rule instantiates it (3552 came so from unit 229's tree root, and its hand edit went: unit 115).

`ledger/callgraph.tsv` and the elem segment give the target's call shapes; each roots file's header says which shape
it reproduces for which function. A vtable is data: no call edge leads to it. Its users are the functions that store
its address point, the `vptr` of `ledger/vtables.json` (the vtable's address + 8); `/usr/bin/grep -rlE 'i32\.const
1599000($|[^0-9])' build/out/splat/pieces/asm` lists `build/out/splat/pieces/asm/comum/10266.wat`, the one function
that loads `comum::asn::CConversorHashArquivo`'s. The grep finds an address loaded as one constant, not one computed
from a base and an offset.

Roots TUs compile with `APP_CXXFLAGS`, like the other stand-ins in `src/app/`: uenux2's flags without
`-fno-strict-aliasing`, and kept at `-O2` where the tree builds at `-O3` (`docs/reference/app-flags.md`). A roots
object that emits TSE inline code meant to match therefore compiles it with other flags than its tree's. The tree is
the one whose object holds the copy the target keeps: where that copy's vtable or typeinfo lies
(`docs/reference/facts/link-order.md` §L7 item 15), else the header's. ecourna's `CBitArray` and `CHKDFSeed` have their
vtables among uenux2 objects' records, and their D2 and D0 match at uenux2's flags and differ at ecourna's (units 03
and 11's fragments give the rows). A copy of the executable TU is at that TU's level
(`docs/reference/facts/levels.md` §V1). A landing pad in the target's body says an `-fexceptions` object's copy, which
no roots object at `APP_CXXFLAGS` gives (`CFile`'s constructor, 517, is out of line in `cfile.cpp`:
`docs/reference/facts/app-flags.md`
§F8 item 3). Compile that code with the tree's flags too and compare the functions in a variant link
(`docs/reference/facts/workflow.md` §W13: the roots object compiled so, scanned with the committed symbol file and built
with the committed matches); an object comparison is a lead only, since binaryen's passes can even a difference out
(`docs/reference/facts/codegen.md` §C1). Say in the fragment's header that they are the same (unit 75's fragment does:
`-fno-strict-aliasing` changes none of its functions). A change of a tree's flags that `APP_CXXFLAGS` does not follow
measures this again for every roots object that supplies a TSE match, and the fragments that state it are rewritten.
When they differ, the tree's flags decide: a match that holds only through the flag its tree lacks is not sanctioned
(`docs/how-to-decompile.md` step 4, "When only a flag closes the gap"). Deleting its line does nothing while the roots'
copy equals the target, since the content pass adds it back, so take the instance out of the roots and keep the
stand-ins' other call sites: the row is then `no-candidate` until a tree object instantiates it, and the fragment says
so with the measurement (5725, unit 113). A unit may also compile its roots at its tree's flags (unit 113, and the
tree root `roots_cbasetype.cpp`); then no match can rest on a flag its tree lacks, and the fragment says so.
`$(UENUX2_CXXFLAGS)` on the rule is the tree's flags only outside `UENUX2_TBAA_DIRS` and in the files
`UENUX2_NOTBAA_FILES` lists: it keeps `-fno-strict-aliasing`, which the directories the split lists drop
(`$(call uenux2_cxxflags,<path>)` gives a directory's or a file's own). A companion at
`$(UENUX2_CXXFLAGS)` that emits code of such a directory compares its object compiled both ways, and says so, when it
lands and at every change of the split (units 97, 113 and 154's roots; `docs/reference/facts/levels.md` §V5 has the
measurement). Taking an instance out is a withdrawal, so measure the fragment's
"differs" forms for that object
again (`docs/how-to-decompile.md` step 10, "Withdrawing a match"). A body a companion object supplies can match only
when it is code a landed header defines: an implicit or inline member, or a template instance such as
`__shared_ptr_emplace<T>`'s destructors. A stand-in's own body or a trap never can. `docs/reference/facts/units.md` §U4
lists the matches that come from companion objects today, and `docs/reference/splat.md` "Reviewing a match" says how a
reviewer checks them.

The order of a roots file's lines is free where it moves data only. It moves the read-only data after it, and with it
the `_ZTS` names a `__get_deleter` compares, but the splat build looks a typeinfo name up at any address
(`docs/reference/facts/splat.md` §S9): a verdict that turns on that is a tool defect to report, not a reason to tune
the order. A tree root that stands for a TU's code is another case: the order of what it instantiates is the order
that TU emits its code in, which can decide a copy's bytes (7667's format string sits at the target's frame offset
only with `roots_texttospeech.cpp`'s sink ahead of its builder). There the root takes the order the TU's data run gives
(its typeinfos and vtables lie in the order its code first refers to them, a reading:
`docs/reference/link-order.md` §2), and its comment gives the run; with no such evidence the order is a choice the
fragment states with both measurements.

**Pending members** (`-pending.cpp`). A class's vtable is emitted in the TU that defines its first non-inline
virtual member, and it needs every virtual member defined, including the ones not reconstructed yet.
`40-uenux2-cprogressbar-pending.cpp` defines those with a body of `__builtin_trap()`: the class links and
the reconstructed constructor can be measured, and a trapping body (one `unreachable`) cannot pass for the target's
code, so no match can land on it. It compiles with the unit's own flags, as the real members will, through a
rule of its own in the fragment (it is not in `ECOURNA_SRCS`) that is added to `EXTRA_OBJS`. Each member
moves into the class's real source when it is reconstructed, and the file and its rule go with the last one.

A trap is for a member that cannot be written yet (a callee or a type the tree lacks, or no form measured comes
close, the `-pending.cpp` header giving the forms and their rows: unit 69's 9018; a slot member one remainder off
stays written), that cannot match until other code lands (a merge thunk, below), or whose real body would cost a
match. A member whose body is written and right keeps it where it belongs, in the
class or its `.cpp`, and its row stays as it is, whatever the reason outside its source: `docs/how-to-decompile.md`
step 5, "A written body that does not match yet", has the rule and what to measure. An implicit destructor has no body
to trap either: declaring it to trap it changes the class. A destructor the D0 shows out of line (step 5, "The
destructors say how the class is declared") has one, and when it needs a member's type the tree lacks it is trapped
like any slot: the head leaves that member out, and nothing may construct the class. Declared first, as step 5 asks,
it is the key function, so the pending object emits the vtable until the destructor lands (`vota::CEleitorVotando`'s
D2, 2483, destroys its +20 member through fn 1468).

A trap stands for its member and also hollows a direct caller: binaryen inlines the one `unreachable` into it, so a
caller that reaches the member directly (a member object's call is devirtualized) keeps no code of its own until the
member lands. The caller stays written, the fragment says so, and what only that caller called needs a root: unit 92's
`CInputField<CFramedText>::Draw` over `CFramedText::MaskText`, and the stand-in that keeps `DrawFrame` (2779).

What the vtable needs, slot by slot:

- **The key function pending.** When the pending member is the class's key function (its first non-inline virtual
  member), the vtable is emitted in the pending object, and with it every implicit or inline member the vtable names
  (an implicit destructor). Those bodies are the compiler's code for the class definition, not stand-ins, and they
  match from the pending object, or from an earlier object that emits them too: a derived class's TU that stores its
  own vptr emits the base's inline destructors with its vtable (`comum::CCopiadorMR`'s D2 comes from
  `ccopiadorwsqmr.cpp.o`), so read `names.tsv`'s object column. They move with the vtable, under the same symbols, when
  the key function lands
  (units 26, 29, 58, 59, 60, 62, 77 and 79; `docs/reference/facts/units.md` §U4).
- **A base's key function.** A class's typeinfo names its base's typeinfo, and a base with a virtual member defined
  out of line emits its typeinfo only in the TU that defines its key function. So when no unit defines the base's
  key function, the link stops on `undefined symbol: typeinfo for <base>`, even when the class overrides every slot
  of the base. Define the base's out-of-line virtual members as traps in the `-pending.cpp` too (its vtable, emitted
  there with the typeinfo, needs every one of them). When nothing in our link stores the base's vptr (a base without
  data members, whose store the derived destructor drops), wasm-ld drops the base's vtable and the traps and keeps the
  typeinfo, and the traps' target functions keep their stubs (§U9). A later unit of another class derived from that
  base uses these traps; it does not define them a second time (`duplicate symbol`).
- **A member inline in the original.** A class with no key function in the target has its vtable emitted with every
  TU that uses it (`docs/reference/facts/reading.md` §R6). When one of its inline members cannot be written yet (its
  callee's class has no header), declare it in the class without a body, trap it in the `-pending.cpp`, and say in the
  class header that the member is inline in the original and moves into the class with its body. The trap is then a
  key function of our link only: the pending object emits the vtable strong where the original emits it weak. The
  inline members it emits with it are the same code either way, so the matches they give do not depend on the trap
  (§U10). They do depend on whose copy the target kept. A class whose typeinfo lies in the executable TU's data
  (`vota_web_wasm.cpp`, first on the TSE link line) has that TU's copies of its inline members, compiled
  `-Oz -fexceptions` (`docs/reference/facts/levels.md` §V1). An inline member that this level compiles otherwise
  than the uenux2 flags matches only from an object at that TU's rule ahead of the tree: the TU's own (unit 170), once
  its code that uses the class is written, or a tree root at its rule that builds the class as that code does
  (`src/app/roots_texttospeech.cpp`, unit 237, for `ITextToSpeech`'s `SetVoice` 11518, destructor 11489 and
  `Synthesize` 11530, `differs` or pending from unit 82's objects until then).
- **A default of an interface with no vtable.** A class with no vtable of its own in the target shows its inline
  defaults only in a derived class's vtable, in a slot no override explains (a
  body that builds a lambda whose RTTI is named after the base's member is the base's). Write each default the
  evidence gives as an inline body in the base's header: it emits nothing until a derived class inherits it. A default
  whose body needs a name not recovered yet stays `= 0`, and the base's header says which slot it is, what the target
  shows and what it waits for, so that the unit that writes the derived class writes it in the base and not as an
  override. Neither a trap in the header nor a member declared out of line: either gives the base a key function, and
  with it a vtable and a typeinfo the target does not have. Unit 74 left `api::ISound`'s slots 8 and 9 `= 0`, and
  unit 81 wrote their bodies in `api/audio/isound.h`. An interface whose one implementer overrides every slot shows
  no default: declare each slot pure in the interface, named from the implementer's body or after its slot, and say in
  its header that the names are chosen (unit 119's `comum::IControladorRegistraMesarios`). A missing row in
  `ledger/vtables.json` does not say the class has no vtable: read the data for one (`docs/reference/facts/units.md`
  §U12).
- **A pure slot.** A slot that holds `__cxa_pure_virtual` (12143) is a pure member. Declare it `= 0` at its slot even
  when no evidence names it: name it from its callers, else after its slot, and say in the class header that the name
  is chosen. Left out, it makes the class concrete, which gives it a deleting destructor the target does not have and
  a shorter vtable (unit 35, §U8).
- **An inherited template default.** A slot the class inherits from a class template without overriding it (the
  default `DoConverte` of `comum::asn::IConversorASN<ENTIDADE, DADO>`) holds that template's member. When its
  definition is not recovered (a stub in the shared header `app/comum/asn/iconversorasn.h`), define the one
  instantiation the vtable needs as an explicit specialization with a trapping body in the `-pending.cpp`, never in
  the shared header, which every converter unit compiles. Such a member never moves: when the template's definition is
  written, delete every such specialization (`LC_ALL=C git grep -n 'template <>' -- 'src/units/*-pending.cpp'`). A
  specialization is a strong definition and the template's instance a weak one, so one left behind keeps its trap in
  the vtable and the link says nothing (§U6).
- **A member whose target body is a merge thunk.** A target function whose whole body is one call into a binaryen
  merge-similar-functions body, with its own constants as arguments (the glossary's mgfn: `CImageField::GetTypeName`,
  11097, is `f2904(p0, p1, 197764, 197771)`), matches when our link merges enough of those functions for binaryen to
  form the same shared body. The merged functions can be other classes' and libraries', and the same operands must
  differ among them, so that the shared body takes the same parameters. Two can be enough for a large body: the
  target's 1565 has eight callers, and our link formed it from units 54's and 56's deleting destructors (3681, 2777). A
  small one needs more: the exit destructors' shapes form from 3 and 4 members (`docs/reference/facts/codegen.md` §C8,
  which has the `wasm-opt` probe to run on N copies before reading the row). `pnpm wasm fn <target> 2904` lists a body's
  callers, and a unit that lands another caller reads its siblings' rows in the scan. Until the
  body forms, the member stays pending, with its source written in the `-pending.cpp` header (unit 29). When every
  member of the target's set is a trap, none forms the body alone, so the unit that writes the last one writes them
  all, or a landing writes them together (10315 and 10453 over 6012 were units 135's and 221's traps until one change
  wrote both, each in its own unit's file, and the three matched). A template
  default (the bullet above) whose target body is a merge thunk takes both: the explicit specialization, and in the
  header its source, with the message and the typeid name its call passes (unit 47's 11360 was one until unit 159 wrote
  the default in its header). It stays a specialization while it also waits for a flag: written at a level that does
  not give the target's code, the default gives a whole body where the target has a thunk, gains no match, and reaches
  every converter of the template. The shared body's own callees need names from elsewhere, since a function an anchor's vote names votes in turn only when
  its body is the same opcodes (`docs/reference/splat.md` §2): a `CBaseError` constructor thunk has its name from
  `rtti-vptr`, the hand edits for 406
  and 1715. The other way round, a member whose source gives the target's body can become a merge thunk in our link
  alone, when binaryen merges it with bodies of like shape that the target keeps apart: `splat-remap` then reports
  `UNRESOLVED` on its one `call`, into a `byn$mgfn-shared$…` body, which no naming step resolves, and it matches once
  those bodies have the target's shape (unit 40's 10975 waited so on 5075).

A function no vtable slot holds is not a pending member: a constructor, or a non-virtual member that other members
call. It is never trapped. A member the link must hold (a vtable slot, a rooted function) whose only unwritten callee
is such a function stays pending itself, with its source in the `-pending.cpp` header. One nothing reaches is written,
its callees declared only, and wasm-ld drops it with what it alone reaches; its fragment names what it waits for (unit
253's `SalvaGeral` and `SalvaApps`, 10243 and 10212, reached only through slots unwritten callers pass). A member a
constructor
would bring (the control block of a `std::make_shared<T>` the target calls) waits for the unit that writes it only
when no builder the link drops can emit it ("A control block").

A pending member has no stub: `tools/round/stubs.py` counts a function in a slot of a vtable our link holds as code
of ours, so `make -C src stubs` removes its stub when the unit lands. It counts a virtual member of a class as well,
with or without a vtable in the link, once a `.cpp` of ours that holds the class's stubs defines one of its members
(`Class::member(` at column 0, `_member_files` in `stubs.py`; `stubs.py --report` gives both counts): 5503,
`comum::IInterfaceSavd`'s destructor, went so, and no TU constructs the class. A class head in a header does not
count, so the first member a unit writes in that `.cpp` takes the stubs of slots it never mentions, and a fragment
that defines a class names each slot it leaves with no body. That second count goes by the class's last name alone,
so it can take another class's stubs: 10874 and 10875
are `api::CAjusteDataHora`'s, and unit 93 defines `comum::md::estadoaplicacao::CAjusteDataHora`. When that stub was the
last one of a stub-only file, the file goes too ([../README.md](../README.md) "Stubs"). The `-pending.cpp` header names each member with its
target index and what it waits for, and the file and record lines of a stub-only file that went with it, since the
member's reconstruction writes that file again. What a member waits for is what is missing (`api/util/itimer.h is
not in this tree yet`, a callee no evidence names), never who is writing it: a round, a lane or an agent's handle
means nothing once the run that used it ends.

A slot our link fills with a body that is not the target's loses its stub the same way, though it is no trap. Such a
body is a defaulted or implicit member a header of an earlier unit holds (unit 69's `CWasmScreen` vtable had
`api::IScreen`'s `~IScreen() = default` in slots 0 and 1, whose target functions call fn 5069). The fragment that brings
the vtable names such a slot, with what it waits for. The body can also take a matched function's symbol-file line
(`docs/reference/facts/splat.md` §S8, the fifth kind).

In the generated files the trap stands for the member. `make -C src splat-symbols` names the function in a slot of a
vtable `ledger/vtables.json` lists after ours (`evidence:vtable`; a class found only from the data gets no such name,
`docs/reference/splat.md` §2), so when the trap is a function of its own the symbol file names the target function
after the trap's declaration, and the scan lists it as `differs` with the `-pending.o` as its object (10841, unit
158's `CRHVoiceTextToSpeech::DoSynthesize`). binaryen merges the traps of one wasm type into one body that fills
several vtables' slots; then no name is given and the member stays `no-candidate` (7481, unit 104's
`CAguardaMensagem::ProcessKey`, until it was written; §U5). A trap a unit adds can merge
with another unit's that was alone, and that member then loses its name and its `differs` row. When a member lands, the
trap it shared a body with is alone again and gains them: 10900, unit 84's `CMenuValidation::isValid`, shared a body
with 11366 until 11366 landed (§U5). So declare the trap with the member's real signature, its record's. When a landed
header forces another one, say so in the `-pending.cpp` header: until that header changes, `splat/symbol_addrs.txt`
carries its name for the target function.

To try a candidate with its pending file before the fragment exists, see `docs/how-to-decompile.md` step 5, "A class
with virtual members": try-tu has no rule for a path under `src/units/`.

**Externs** (`-externs.cpp`). Definitions a unit calls that belong to a unit not landed yet:
`12-ecourna-api-sql-sqlite-externs.cpp` holds `CStringUtils::ToLower` and `Replace`, reconstructed from their target
functions (5158, 1879, 9398) and compiled with the ecourna flags (listed in `ECOURNA_SRCS` as `../units/…`). Unlike a
pending member, an extern is a real reconstruction; it moves into the owning source (`api/util/cstringutils.cpp`)
when that unit lands. They are in our link once unit 12's roots file reaches the DAO that calls them. `Replace`
(9398) and the two `ToLower` (5158, 1879) match, and the file's header says what each rests on. Unit 00 already compiles
`api/util/cstringutils.cpp`, so by "Landing a unit" item 2 these belong in that source, as an extension of unit 00.
The externs file holds them until a unit moves them there. A new unit adds no extern for a file a landed unit
compiles: it extends that unit.

## Landing a unit

1. Measure the candidate TUs with `tools/round/try-tu.sh` (the whole link; `--single` for one function) until the
   target functions you expect pair with your symbols. Once the sources, the fragment and its companions are where
   they land, steps 6 to 9 of `docs/how-to-decompile.md` measure the same pairing on the real link (step 9's match
   lines; step 7's `moved:` list holds tier changes only, so an empty one says nothing); a separate try is then for comparing variants without touching the tree
   (`--skip-units <your fragment>.mk`).
2. Put the sources under `src/ecourna/` or `src/uenux2/` at the path the target's `std::source_location` records
   name (`docs/how-to-decompile.md` step 3 when none does), and write `src/units/<NN>-<component>-<what>.mk` with
   the next free `NN` (or the one you reserved, "Names"). Its header comment says why the files are where they are
   when no record names them. Check that an edit of each source rebuilds it ("What a fragment holds" item 3).

   A function whose file a landed unit already compiles extends that unit instead: write it into the source, and
   update that fragment's header comment, its roots and pending files and its row in the table. A second fragment
   cannot list the same source (the object would be linked twice), and a fragment of its own for the new function's
   roots would split them from the source they belong to. Try the source with `--skip-units <that fragment>.mk`, or
   the link fails with `duplicate symbol` (unit 18 grew `CThread::Start` this way). A unit that compiles unrelated
   classes' sources (unit 110) can be extended by two people at once, each writing only their own source and their own
   paragraph of the fragment; one with a fragment of its own keeps its members' stand-ins there and says so in that
   paragraph (2803's are in unit 178's roots), and a landing may move them into the unit's roots. Each keeps to its
   own hunks: a stage that extends a unit another stage also extends or claims writes its source, its paragraph and
   the row of the fragment that holds its stand-ins, and says so in its claim; the extended unit's row changes at the
   landing, which folds the members in. A stage with no source of its own may hold such stand-ins in a fragment that
   builds only a roots file, whose header lists each member, its unit and the paragraph with its evidence (unit 212).
   In a TSE header two stages extend, each appends its note to the header comment as a paragraph of its own
   ([../README.md](../README.md) "Gaps"). The rule is the source file's: a
   root that keeps a header template's instance goes with the unit that keeps that template's instances, and the
   source's fragment names it, unless that unit's roots compile at other flags than the object whose copy the target
   keeps: then the instance goes in the roots of a unit at that object's flags, its own or a tree root, and the
   owner's fragment says which (2279 and 2280, `CPolySingleton` and `DeserializeFromFile` instances, in unit 293's
   roots at `UENUX2_CXXFLAGS`, where units 100 and 03 compile theirs at `APP_CXXFLAGS`). A caller stand-in for a
   library instance another unit's object emits follows that object: it goes in that unit's roots, or in a fragment
   of one's own that says whose object the body is while another stage holds that unit. A member with no target index
   of its own (binaryen inlined it into each caller) is
   noted in its class header and in the fragment whose functions hold it; the fragment that compiles its file needs
   only a pointer. Extending a unit brings into the link a function nothing linked before, which can hold a throw off
   its record's line (`CTickManager::SearchNextId` threw on line 89 against its record's 128): compare the file's
   throws with their records, and after the link run `check_srcloc.py --strict` on the object
   (`docs/how-to-decompile.md` step 10).
3. Add a roots file if nothing in the skeleton calls the unit, following the shapes above, and a pending file if
   the class's vtable needs members you have not written.
4. Add the fragment's row to the table under "Names", in its numeric order (100 after 99, not the text order make
   reads the fragments in).
5. Link, census, symbols, match and build, in the order and with the reasons `docs/how-to-decompile.md` steps 6 to
   10 give, and commit the fragment, its companions and its table row with them.

## Switches

- `SKIP_UNITS="<fragment>.mk …"` on the make command line leaves those fragments out; `tools/round/try-tu.sh
  --skip-units` uses it to re-try TUs a landed fragment already links.
- `DADOS_ASN_TRY=1`, `DADOS_MIDIAS_TRY=1`, `DADOS_RUC_ASN_TRY=1` in the environment leave the units of fragments 04,
  08 and 09 and their roots out (04 keeps the ASN.1 modules), so try-tu can compile the same TUs as candidates.
